    29	\title{When Does Admission Control Pay?\\
    30	The Memory Tier Sets the Cost of Concurrency in Agentic LLM Serving}
    31	
    32	\author{\IEEEauthorblockN{Anonymous Submission}}
    33	
    34	\maketitle
    35	
    36	\begin{abstract}
    37	Agentic LLM sessions are long, multi-round jobs whose KV footprint grows every
    38	round, and on a memory-constrained accelerator the pool is the binding
    39	resource. Admission control therefore looks like the obvious lever: a
    40	controller that reads engine telemetry and admits on measured capacity should
    41	beat a static cap. We give a measurement that says how much
    42	room there is, and show what happens when you build against it. Throughput during busy time is
    43	$\overline{R}/\tau$---running requests over per-step cost---so raising
    44	concurrency by a factor $f$ buys $f^{1-\epsilon}$, where
    45	$\epsilon = \mathrm{d}\ln\tau / \mathrm{d}\ln\overline{R}$. Near zero, busy-time throughput keeps
    46	converting; near one it does not. It is a property of the workload \emph{and}
    47	the engine configuration, not of the controller, and what sets it is the
    48	memory tier. The trace bounds reuse at 75.5\% of its prompt tokens, and no
    49	configuration we ran reaches that bound---the best realizes 92.5\% of it.
    50	What the tier decides is how much of the bound survives added concurrency:
    51	device-only, realization falls from 92.5\% to 36.4\% as the running count
    52	rises from 1.00 to 1.84 and the shortfall appears as recomputation (8.6\,M to
    53	20.7\,M tokens); with a host tier the static points hold at 90.2--92.4\%
    54	between 1.25 and 1.73, and across the nineteen host-tier runs that completed
    55	this trace realization never falls below 72.8\%, while the \emph{device} cache
    56	misses more. Two configurations at the
    57	same running count and the same pool occupancy realize 79.0\% and 91.3\% of
    58	the bound, so it is not how full the pool is but what happens when it
    59	overflows---though that pair is two runs on two days, so we also have a
    60	capacity control in the queue that changes the pool size alone on one card; we
    61	report it when it lands. Our controllers run on the host-tier configuration, where
    62	$\epsilon$ stays below one across their whole operating range, so the ceiling
    63	we measure on device-only caps is not what bounded them. It bounds what added concurrency is worth, not what a
    64	whole run achieves---idle time and the SLO axis are separate. We measure $\epsilon$ on a
    65	memory-bound agentic replay (200 sessions, one 24\,GB GPU, a pool that holds
    66	four median sessions) and get $0.38$ to $0.61$ depending on which step cost a
    67	system can observe: every 10\% of added concurrency buys 4--6\% of throughput.
    68	A second, real agent trace (117 Claude sessions, same engine and pool)
    69	reproduces both measurements in direction, neither in level: realization falls
    70	there too, and the elasticity there also turns positive---rising to 1.0 where
    71	the main trace's reaches 1.15---at a different running count and by an amount
    72	our own perturbation study cannot pin down.
    73	
    74	The verdict on the controllers is negative, and we report it at that
    75	strength. (The criterion of \S\ref{sec:criterion} is a diagnostic and does
    76	not return this verdict; \S\ref{sec:criterion} is explicit that it does not
    77	explain the failure.) Nine controller variants spanning four routes, plus a
    78	reimplementation of an independent congestion-based controller, none is
    79	above the static Pareto front at the latency threshold interactive serving
    80	cares about---the best misses it by under a point, on the below side of our
    81	tie band, and the closest arm we repeated misses by 0.9 with its repeat at 5.6;
    82	a plain cap on resident sessions
    83	remains the fastest policy we measured. That is not an argument for admitting everything: with no control at
    84	all the median request waits 1{,}774 seconds for its first token, the prefix
    85	cache's realized hit rate collapses from the trace's planned 75.5\% to
    86	1.6\% (both are the runner's own end-of-run summary figures, on its own
    87	all-steps convention; \texttt{nocontrol.csv} carries them alongside four
    88	conventions we compute ourselves, which give 73.8\% and 1.9\% for the same
    89	run), and SLO falls to 3\%.
    90	The question is which selective rule, not whether to be selective. Two
    91	measurable effects, rather than one we can tune away, account for part of why
    92	our variants fail---the arm-by-arm attribution of
    93	\S\ref{sec:results:attribution} attributes four of the twelve controller
    94	arms we tested, leaves five with a step cost that no matched comparison can
    95	confirm, and leaves three as residuals we can measure and cannot yet
    96	explain. The single step-cost attribution it does make is on a run flagged
    97	for host co-tenancy but not measurably damaged by it, and we say which is
    98	which rather than counting it. They hold
    99	nearly half
   100	the pool idle while sessions queue---their own headroom estimate on the base
   101	configuration is 1.79$\times$ the truth (1.32--1.90$\times$ across the
   102	sixteen controller runs that finished on this trace, and 1.01--4.11$\times$
   103	over every finished controller run we logged)---and the per-step elasticity measured on the
   104	configuration they actually run stays below one across their whole operating
   105	range, so the engine had room they did not take: the ceiling we measure on
   106	device-only caps does not survive on the host-tier configuration the
   107	controllers use. The wall
   108	clock splits into busy and idle time; admission policy moves only the running
   109	count, and $\tau$ rises with it. The idle component (7.3--41.9\% of the
   110	sampled span) comes from gaps the tool waits set---an agent session issues no
   111	request while a tool runs,
   112	and that wait averages 16.85\,s measured over the controller's run, against the
   113	15.82\,s the trace demands---on the static cap of one the two match exactly, so
   114	we attribute the controller's extra 1.03\,s to its own admission delay---not
   115	the sub-second figure the trace's median suggests---though how many such gaps a run collects is partly the policy's,
   116	since a policy sets how many sessions stay live. On device-only configurations we can account for the rise token for
   117	token; on host-tier ones part of it is co-tenancy we found late and part we
   118	still cannot attribute, and we say which is which. We also report the six of
   119	our own claims we retracted along the way, and the boundary of the verdict: at
   120	a loose latency threshold one variant does cross the front, by a margin we
   121	cannot resolve against run-to-run noise.
   122	\end{abstract}
   123	
   124	\begin{IEEEkeywords}
   125	LLM serving, admission control, KV cache, agentic workloads, performance

   311	
   312	Device occupancy does not explain the split. \texttt{cap3\_fixed} and
   313	\texttt{cap3\_hc} run at nearly the same mean device occupancy (55.8\% and
   314	55.5\%) and nearly the same running count (1.53 and 1.51), and they realize
   315	79.0\% and 91.3\% of the bound respectively. What differs is not how full the
   316	device pool is but what happens when it overflows: with no host tier an
   317	evicted prefix is gone and must be recomputed; with one it is demoted and read
   318	back. The pool is the binding resource, the tier sets the pool, and the tier
   319	therefore sets whether added concurrency is paid for in recomputation. The
   320	caveat is that this pair is two runs on two different days and two different
   321	GPUs, not a paired experiment; \S\ref{sec:repro} states what we did to rule
   322	out co-tenancy. A capacity-controlled version---same card, same trace, only
   323	the pool size changed---is queued behind the re-runs and we report it in
   324	\S\ref{sec:results} when it lands; we do not use it here.
   325	
   326	\paragraph{The second trace agrees on direction and not on level.}
   327	We ran the same accounting on a second workload, reported in full in
   328	\S\ref{sec:xtrace}---117 real Claude agent sessions, 390 rounds, the same pool
   329	and the same engine---and it reproduces the direction: its realization of \emph{its own} bound falls from 74.8\% to 57.5\%
   330	as the running count rises from 1.08 to 1.50, and over that span the whole-range
   331	per-unit slope is $-40.9$ points, against the device-only family's $-66.8$ over
   332	$\overline{R}_{\text{busy}}$ 1.00--1.84 (\texttt{slope.csv}). What it does not reproduce is the level, and this is
   333	worth stating because it bounds what we can claim. The lightest cap on that
   334	trace (\texttt{realhr\_cap2}) runs at a \emph{lower} median device occupancy
   335	than the lightest on this one (15.8\% against 22.4\%, \texttt{cap1}) and
   336	realizes \emph{less} (74.8\% against 92.5\%), so realization is not a function
   337	of occupancy alone---and a
   338	statement of the form ``the pool collapses above $x$\% occupancy'' is wrong on
   339	our own data. Occupancy is instantaneous while reuse depends on history; a
   340	session's prefix must survive from one round to the next, and how much
   341	pressure that puts on the pool is not visible in what the pool holds at an
   342	instant. We do not have the measurement that would separate those, which is
   343	why the controlled experiment we report in \S\ref{sec:results} varies the
   344	pool alone, on one card and one trace, rather than leaning on the cross-trace
   345	comparison. The claim this section supports is the narrow one: within a
   346	configuration, the host tier preserves realization at a matched running count
   347	and a matched occupancy, and without it realization falls.
   348	
   349	That gives the criterion of \S\ref{sec:criterion} a mechanism rather than a
   350	correlation, and it suggests a prediction we are testing:
   351	if the tier acts by multiplying usable capacity (the host pool is twice the
   352	device pool, so three times in total), then shrinking the device pool alone
   353	should move the collapse point even with no host tier involved. We do not
   354	treat the arithmetic as a result---it assumes KV demand grows linearly in the
   355	running count, which heterogeneous sessions need not satisfy---and the
   356	queued experiment measures the collapse point directly rather than predicting
   357	it, and \S\ref{sec:results} reports it once it has run.
   358

   342	\paragraph{Reproducibility.}
   343	\label{sec:repro}
   344	The front and the signed distance of each controller are computed by
   345	\texttt{scripts/lab/frontier.py} from the raw per-step logs; Table~\ref{tab:runs}
   346	is rendered by \texttt{scripts/lab/gen\_table.py} from
   347	\texttt{frontier.py}'s full-precision coordinates
   348	(\texttt{paper/data/runs\_precise.csv}), including the four runs that stopped
   349	short of 999 steps; the live-session column comes from the runner's admission
   350	log via \texttt{run\_summary.py}, and \S\ref{sec:criterion} is computed by
   351	\texttt{gen\_elasticity.py} and \texttt{gen\_elasticity\_hc.py}. Every run is
   352	guarded against a shrunken KV pool.
   353	
   354	\emph{Co-tenancy on the measurement host.} Our runs share a host with other
   355	tenants and with each other, and late in the campaign we found that this
   356	matters more than the pool guard catches. An audit
   357	(\texttt{gen\_concurrency.py}, data in \texttt{paper/data/concurrency.csv})
   358	reconstructs, for every run, how many other runs were alive for how long. Runs
   359	that overlapped three or more others for at least an hour are flagged;
   360	fourteen of ours are, two of which are failed runs we do not report. The flag is a risk marker, not a measurement of damage,
   361	and we treat it that way: five of the twelve (\texttt{F:t85}, \texttt{F:t90},
   362	\texttt{var\_foyer}, \texttt{cap1} and the published-parameter AIMD row) show
   363	no aggregate inflation at all, because their overlap was short relative to the
   364	run or did not touch their decode interval. Where the damage is legible it is large---the
   365	per-request decode interval $\tau_2$, measured client-side and never touching
   366	the sampler, sits at 30.9--33.3\,ms for the three affected arms still carrying
   367	the flag in
   368	Table~\ref{tab:runs} against 23.7--25.0\,ms across the seven clean runs of the
   369	same controller family---a 24 to 41\% inflation. A fourth affected arm has
   370	since been re-measured alone and came in at
   371	24.8\,ms, inside the clean range, which is the correction in action. Two
   372	controls pin the cause to co-tenancy rather than to the configuration, and both
   373	are within-run---the same run, the same policy, the same trace, with the only
   374	change being who else was on the machine. One run (\texttt{cap1}) reads a
   375	per-half-hour median $\tau_2$ of 20.3--20.9\,ms for its first five and a half
   376	hours, and 21.3, 27.1 and 21.1 in the three half-hours after two others start.
   377	Two runs move the other way as neighbours leave: \texttt{F:t85} from 29.8\,ms
   378	in the half-hour before to 22.3--22.6 in the four after, \texttt{F:rl99} from
   379	32.4 to 25.2 and 25.1. We chose the half-hour window because it is the widest
   380	one that resolves the transition and still puts at least twenty steps in every
   381	bucket quoted; the finer series is noisier and does not support a
   382	minute-resolution claim, so we do not make one.
   383	(\texttt{tau2\_windows.csv} carries the series.) Fourteen of our runs fall in the flagged class; five of them are rows of
   384	Table~\ref{tab:runs} (\texttt{cap1}, \texttt{F:hz0},

   401	\subsection{Route 1: the accounting base}
   402	
   403	Our controller's projection (Eq.~\ref{eq:proj}) charges a session for its full
   404	context even while it is parked in a tool wait, when its KV is evictable and
   405	therefore absent from the engine's reported occupancy. We argued this was a
   406	double charge and removed it, admitting on engine telemetry alone.
   407	
   408	The charge is not double, and removing it does what the argument predicted on
   409	the engine side: median occupancy rises from 49.2\% to 56.2\% of the pool and
   410	the busy-hour running count from 1.35 to 1.48, and the round-1+ prefix hit
   411	rate falls only from 80.6\% to 79.7\%, so no eviction spiral occurred. The run
   412	also finishes 25.4 minutes \emph{earlier} (265.9 against 291.3).
   413	
   414	It is nonetheless not a win. The SLO falls from 89.8\% to 85.9\%---3.90\,pp---
   415	and the static front itself falls 3.81\,pp over those same 25.4 minutes, so the
   416	arm keeps its place relative to the envelope to within 0.09\,pp while moving
   417	25 minutes along it: it slides, at the front's own slope, rather than
   418	crossing. What makes that a test rather than a coincidence is what it would
   419	have taken to cross: at 265.9 minutes the front sits at 87.2\%, so the arm
   420	would have had to give up no more than 2.6\,pp of SLO instead of 3.9, and it
   421	did not.

    86	
    87	The verdict criterion deserves care, because ``faster than cap-$N$'' is not
    88	the question. The question is whether a policy reaches a point \emph{above}
    89	the static front. A point that lies merely between two static caps is not a
    90	win: mixing cap-$N$ and cap-$M$ by time slice approximates any point on the
    91	line between them. We flag this segment argument as an argument, not a
    92	measurement---we did not run the time-sliced policies, and carrying queue state
    93	and cache contents across a switch could bend the line. It is the criterion we
    94	apply, and it is the stricter of the two we could have used---stricter than
    95	comparing each controller against the discrete static points alone, because the
    96	line sits above them. We state the direction plainly rather than claim it as a
    97	caution: this choice makes our negative result \emph{easier} to reach, not
    98	harder. Our best arm is $0.93$\,pp below the line and would be $+3.7$\,pp
    99	against the nearest static point below it in wall clock (\texttt{cap3}+HiCache's
   100	first run, 81.1\% at 236.4 minutes; $+2.9$ would be the comparison against that
   101	anchor's repeat at 81.9\%, which is the one we displaced it with). So we take the
   102	upper-left
   103	envelope of the static points and report each controller's signed distance
   104	above or below it. We classify a signed distance of more than $+1.0$\,pp as
   105	above the front, less than $-1.0$\,pp as below it, and the band between the
   106	two as \emph{on} it---a tie, not a win. The band is a convention we fixed

    38	\texttt{cap4}+HC, over $\overline{R}_{\text{busy}}$ 1.25 to 1.73. That range
    39	covers the whole controller operating region (1.35 to 1.69), and over it
    40	$\epsilon$ never approaches one. At matched running counts the HiCache caps pay
    41	$1.7$\,ms less per step at $\overline{R}_{\text{busy}} \approx 1.5$.
    42	
    43	This contradicts what an earlier draft of this paper claimed, and it changes
    44	what the criterion is for. The cliff in the device-only curve is a
    45	recomputation effect: \S\ref{sec:model} measures that those runs recompute what
    46	they evict, while under HiCache the host tier absorbs it. The cliff does not
    47	survive on the configuration our controllers actually use. So the criterion
    48	does \emph{not} explain why they lose---on their own configuration they were
    49	not in a region where added concurrency was self-defeating, and the engine had
    50	room they did not take. What the measurement does is rule one explanation out.
    51	The failure is not that this workload denies admission control its lever; it is
    52	that our controllers do not pull the lever they have, which is what
    53	\S\ref{sec:results:idle-memory} and the attribution of
    54	\S\ref{sec:results:attribution} measure directly.
    55	
    56	We are explicit about what this does and does not establish. The HiCache curve
    57	rests on three configurations and two segments, one run each, with no
    58	error bars, and it stops at 1.73---we cannot see whether a cliff appears
    59	beyond the controllers' region. A $\pm10\%$ perturbation of $\tau$ puts the
    60	segment elasticities' bounds across one even on the device-only curve, so the
    61	cliff is a description of the fitted points rather than a threshold we have
    62	located precisely. And it is measured on
    63	different policies rather than by an intervention, so it is a
    64	cross-policy association. On our data it is also computed from the same wall
    65	clock the model then explains---$\tau_1$ is derived from $W_{\text{busy}}$, so
    66	using it to predict $W_{\text{busy}}$ restates rather than tests---and its
    67	points are different policies, so the slope is a cross-policy association, not
    68	a transportable causal derivative. $\tau_2$ is genuinely independent of the
    69	wall clock but measures only decode, so it cannot be dropped into a throughput
    70	equation that includes prefill.
    71	
    72	We tested whether the curve transfers to a second workload. On a real agent
    73	trace (117 sessions, median inter-round

codex
我按第 15 轮的四条 major 和两处追溯缺口核对了现稿。结论是：**M1 已解决；M2、M3、M4 仍是部分解决；两处 CSV 的数字已补上，但重复实验的正文口径还有一处错误。**

| 项目 | 判定 | 核对结果 |
|---|---|---|
| **M1：bres 旧结论** | **RESOLVED** | [§III 末段](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:441)已写为空转减少 9.5 分钟、忙时减少 15.5 分钟、墙钟提前 25.4 分钟；[§V](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:408)也报告提前 25.4 分钟但 SLO 下降 3.9 pp、相对前沿的位置基本未变。原来“整体更慢”的结论已撤回。 |
| **M2：九条判决含三条不可比测量** | **PARTIALLY** | [§V 新段](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:17)确实把三条标为暂定，说明修正方向有利于控制器，并写了若有一条越线就改成“六条干净、一条胜出”。但同节[开头](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:10)、[摘要](/root/paper/foyer/paper/main.tex:74)和[引言](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_intro.tex:26)仍直接说九条无一越线。限定段落不能让这些无条件句同时成立。 |
| **M3：梯队因果主张与缺失对照** | **PARTIALLY** | [摘要](/root/paper/foyer/paper/main.tex:56)和[§III](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:312)都明写了两次运行跨日期、跨 GPU，且同卡容量对照仍排队。**§IV 没有作者声称的这项具体交代**；它只在[第 56–68 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_criterion.tex:56)给出单次测量、跨策略关联等一般限定。更关键的是，§III [第 318–319 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:318)仍用“therefore sets”作因果表述。披露混淆有进展，但未完成关键对照时不足以支撑这个强度。 |
| **M4：插值线与实测静态策略支配** | **PARTIALLY** | [判据定义](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_background.tex:87)明确承认分时混合只是论证、没有实测，且最佳控制器相对某个静态实测点可以高出 2.9 pp。[图 1 图注](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_intro.tex:39)也解释了连线；但同一句仍写“低于或在线上就没有胜过 *a static cap*”。[摘要](/root/paper/foyer/paper/main.tex:77)只说“static Pareto front”，没有点明插值判据。现稿没有直说“被某个实测点支配”，但图注仍容易让读者这样理解。 |
| **§VI 重复散布追溯** | **PARTIALLY** | [CSV](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/repeat_spread.csv:25)有 1.59%，[第 28 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/repeat_spread.csv:28)有 0.02%；[正文](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_xtrace.tex:123)的 1.6%、0.02%及 0.02–6.08%均有数据对应。但[第 131 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_xtrace.tex:131)称“ten pairs”不准确：十个是**分组**，其中主 trace 基线[这一组](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/repeat_spread.csv:16)有四条 run。§V [第 33–37 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_results.tex:33)提到四条，§VI 这句仍需跟上。 |
| **§repro 分时段 τ₂** | **RESOLVED** | [正文](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_discussion.tex:374)已改为半小时桶中位数。[CSV](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/tau2_windows.csv:7)中，cap1 的前 5.5 小时为 20.3–20.9 ms，随后三桶为 21.3、27.1、21.1；F:t85 的 29.8→22.3–22.6 见[第 29–33 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/tau2_windows.csv:29)，F:rl99 的 32.4→25.2、25.1 见[第 46–48 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/data/tau2_windows.csv:46)。这些被引用的数都在表里。 |

**改动后的连锁问题。**除上述“ten pairs”和仍未同步收窄的九条总判决外，[§III 第 289 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:289)在一个已结束的句子后直接以小写 `while` 开头，是明显的文本断裂；[第 343–357 行](/root/paper/foyer/paper/sec_model.tex:343)还把尚在排队的容量实验写成“§V 报告”的口吻。仅凭现有文件我无法证明它们是哪次修改引入的，但投稿前需要改。

**最挡中稿的是 M2。**三条被作者自己判为不可比的结果仍出现在摘要和开篇的“九条无一胜出”总判决中，而一次已完成的重测曾把距离提高约 6.5 pp。这直接影响论文最醒目的经验结论。M3 的因果强度是紧随其后的障碍。

**按现状投稿，我的判断区间仍是 5–15%。**这是基于现稿论证完整度的审稿判断，**不是录用概率预测**，也不是虚构的审稿分数。
